(As-built through earlier in the day on 22 Sep 2026 had incorrectly contained 100.64.0.0/16 and 127.16.0.0/12, both operator-confirmed typos from original alias creation.) | Active destination block. Runtime table re-pulled via Firewall → Diagnostics → Aliases after the operator applied the fix; all five entries confirmed correct. See §11.1 step 2 (closed) and §8.5 for the required Save → Apply → verify sequence used. |

### 7.4 Corrected active-purpose interface policy

The September policy amendment supersedes RUN-002 v1.31's baseline Internet deny. CYBER_ATTACK, CYBER_WINDOWS, CYBER_LINUX, CYBER_APPS, CYBER_SIEM, and CYBER_ANALYSIS use the following manual IPv4 order beneath the automatic service rules. **Rule order confirmed PASS on all six zones via GUI screenshots, 22 Sep 2026.**

| Order | Action | Source | Destination / port | Logging | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pass TCP/UDP | Interface network | This Firewall, domain 53 | Normally off | Allow DNS to OPNsense |
| 2 | Block IPv4 any | Interface network | This Firewall, any | On | Block other OPNsense control-plane access |
| 3 | Block IPv4 any | Interface network | PROTECTED_NETWORKS, any | On | Block trusted, private, inter-zone, CGNAT/Tailscale, and link-local |
| 4 | Pass IPv4 any | Interface network | Any | Interface-specific | Allow public Internet through OPNsense and automatic Source NAT |
| Default | Implicit deny | Any | Any | Platform default | Block traffic not matched above |

**Critical rule order:** The Internet pass must remain below both block rules. Do not move it above PROTECTED_NETWORKS. Do not rename working rules solely for cosmetic consistency during active buildout.

**Note:** rule *order* is correct on all six zones. Rule 3's effectiveness was reduced earlier on 22 Sep 2026 by the now-corrected PROTECTED_NETWORKS alias content issue described in §7.3 (a block rule can only be as good as what its alias actually contains); with the alias corrected and re-verified, rule 3 provides its full intended coverage.

### 7.4.1 First inter-VLAN attack-path exception — CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_LINUX (3 Oct 2026)

§7.4's `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block (which includes `10.0.0.0/8`) was, by design, blocking every cyber zone from reaching every other cyber zone. This had only ever been confirmed in the single-destination sense (e.g. VM310 correctly blocked from the Proxmox host during the VM331 build) — it had never been tested zone-to-zone until the VM334 build, the first Linux-zone target actually reachability-tested from the attack zone, surfaced that kali-attack (VM310, CYBER_ATTACK) could not reach VM334 (CYBER_LINUX) on any port. This was the expected effect of existing, already-verified policy, not a bug — but it meant the range had no working attack-to-target path at all, on any zone, because inter-zone traffic had simply never been attempted before.

**Operator's design decision, 3 Oct 2026:** add explicit, narrowly-scoped pass rules per target zone rather than one blanket exception — preserving full port range per zone (so reconnaissance/port-discovery stays part of the exercise, rather than the firewall pre-filtering to only the "known vulnerable" ports) while keeping SIEM (VLAN 50) and Analysis (VLAN 60) blocked from the attack zone. VLAN 20 (Windows) and VLAN 40 (Apps) will get identical rules once those zones have real hosts — this is the template.

New rule, added to `CYBER_ATTACK` only, positioned above the zone's existing `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block and below its control-plane block:

| Order | Action | Source | Destination / port | Logging | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Pass IPv4 any | Interface network | CYBER_LINUX net, any | On | Attack-path exception: CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_LINUX (VLAN 30) target zone |

This renumbers the existing `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block to order 4 and the Internet pass to order 5, **on the CYBER_ATTACK interface only**. CYBER_WINDOWS, CYBER_LINUX, CYBER_APPS, CYBER_SIEM, and CYBER_ANALYSIS are unaffected and retain the original four-rule order from §7.4.

**Second layer required, discovered by the same test:** the OPNsense rule alone was not sufficient — VM334's own host firewall (UFW, configured SSH-only during its own hardening pass, before Jenkins/GitLab existed) was independently dropping the same traffic. Opening `80/tcp` and `8080/tcp` in UFW on VM334 was also required before either service became reachable. This confirms defense-in-depth is working correctly at both the network (OPNsense) and host (UFW) layers independently — a good sign, not a flaw — but it means every future target host will need its own host-firewall opened for whatever service ports it's meant to expose, on top of any OPNsense zone-level exception. Worth checking for on every future Linux/Windows target in this and other zones.

**Validated 3 Oct 2026** from kali-attack (VM310): GitLab (`302` → `/users/sign_in`) and Jenkins (`403`, `X-Jenkins: 2.426.2` header) both reachable; OPNsense control plane (`10.10.30.1`) still correctly timed out, confirming the exception is scoped exactly as intended.

### 7.4.2 Second inter-VLAN attack-path exception — CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_WINDOWS (4 Oct 2026)

Anticipated by §7.4.1's own note ("VLAN 20 ... will get identical rules once those zones have real hosts") and raised proactively by the operator before starting VM340: with DC01 (VM320) and its domain-joined clients (VM321/322) already built, the same `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` blanket block was also preventing kali-attack from reaching CYBER_WINDOWS — meaning none of the AD lab's planned attack content (Kerberoasting, AS-REP roasting, etc. — see §11.3.1a) was actually reachable from the attack zone yet, same root cause as §7.4.1, just not yet exercised for this zone.

Same template as §7.4.1, added to `CYBER_ATTACK` only, directly below the existing CYBER_LINUX pass rule:

| Order | Action | Source | Destination / port | Logging | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Pass IPv4 any | Interface network | CYBER_WINDOWS net, any | On | Attack-path exception: CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_WINDOWS (VLAN 20) target zone |

This renumbers `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` to order 5 and the Internet pass to order 6, **on the CYBER_ATTACK interface only**. All other zones are unaffected.

**Validated 4 Oct 2026** from kali-attack (VM310): OPNsense control plane (`10.10.20.1:443`) still correctly blocked. `nmap -Pn -p 88,135,389,445,636,3268,3389 10.10.20.10` against DC01 (static IP) showed Kerberos (88), msrpc (135), LDAP (389), microsoft-ds/SMB (445), LDAPS (636), and Global Catalog LDAP (3268) all **open**; RDP (3389) **filtered** — consistent with Windows Defender Firewall acting as a second host-layer control on the domain controller, the same defense-in-depth pattern found on VM334's UFW in §7.4.1, and expected/realistic for a DC rather than a gap to open further. CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_WINDOWS is now a working attack path for the AD lab content.

**Both VLAN 20 and VLAN 30 target zones now have working attack-path exceptions. VLAN 40 (Apps) remains the template's next application, once VM340 has real hosts — see §11.3.1b.**

### 7.4.3 Third inter-VLAN attack-path exception — CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_APPS (4 Oct 2026)

Same template applied proactively this time, as part of VM340's own build plan rather than discovered after the fact — the operator's §7.4.2 catch made this a standard step for any new target zone going forward. Added to `CYBER_ATTACK` only, directly below the existing CYBER_WINDOWS pass rule:

| Order | Action | Source | Destination / port | Logging | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | Pass IPv4 any | Interface network | CYBER_APPS net, any | On | Attack-path exception: CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_APPS (VLAN 40) target zone |

This renumbers `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` to order 6 and the Internet pass to order 7, **on the CYBER_ATTACK interface only**. All other zones are unaffected.

**Validated 4 Oct 2026** from kali-attack (VM310): OPNsense control plane (`10.10.40.1:443`) still correctly blocked. DVWA (`http://10.10.40.121/`) returned `302` (expected — fresh container redirecting to its own `/setup.php` before the database is initialized, not a reachability problem) and Juice Shop (`http://10.10.40.121:3000/`) returned `200`. No host-firewall opening was needed beyond the UFW rules already added for ports 80/3000 during the build itself (unlike VM334/VM335, which needed the host-firewall gap found and closed after the fact) — this zone's target host had its service ports opened as part of the normal build sequence, not discovered missing afterward.

**All three target zones built so far (VLAN 20, VLAN 30, VLAN 40) now have working attack paths.** The template was then applied a fourth time to VLAN 70 (CYBER_HYBRID) as part of that zone's own infrastructure build — see new §7.4.4 — rather than waiting for a guest to surface the gap.

### 7.4.4 Fourth inter-VLAN attack-path exception — CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID (4 Oct 2026)

Added as part of VLAN 70's own infrastructure build (§11.3.1c), not discovered missing after the fact — the fourth consecutive application of the template first established in §7.4.1. Added to `CYBER_ATTACK` only, directly below the existing CYBER_APPS pass rule:

| Order | Action | Source | Destination / port | Logging | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | Pass IPv4 any | Interface network | CYBER_HYBRID net, any | On | Attack-path exception: CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID (VLAN 70) target zone |

This renumbers `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` to order 7 and the Internet pass to order 8, **on the CYBER_ATTACK interface only**. All other zones are unaffected.

**All four target zones built so far (VLAN 20, 30, 40, 70) now have working attack-path exceptions**, all added as a standard part of each zone's own build from here forward. **Full positive validation for CYBER_HYBRID is now complete (5 Oct 2026)** — see §11.3.1g: ping, curl to all three VM370 vhosts, and gobuster against two of them, all clean from kali-attack. This was also the first real live-host test of this rule since it was written by inspection only; it surfaced and helped diagnose an unrelated VM370 networking defect (missing static IP on the VLAN70-facing NIC) before passing clean.

**VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL) is an intentional exception to this pattern — it does not and will not get a §7.4.x entry.** Built 4 Oct 2026 as part of the VM370/371 capstone scenario (§11.3.1d/§11.3.1e), it deliberately receives **no** CYBER_ATTACK pass rule: the zone is reachable only through VM370's own dual-homed NIC and a chisel tunnel, never via a direct OPNsense rule. Its only inter-zone rule is a narrow outbound pass from CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL to CYBER_WINDOWS, AD ports only — see §11.3.1e for the rule build (including a real port-alias correction caught before it could silently break LDAP/Kerberos-over-TCP).

### 7.5 Quarantine policy

CYBER_QUARANTINE remains intentionally closed. Its manual baseline has a logged block to This Firewall and a logged private/inter-zone block, followed by implicit default deny. It has no manual DNS pass and no Internet pass. Automatic DHCP may still assign 10.10.99.100-199. **Confirmed via GUI 22 Sep 2026: second rule destination is RFC1918_NETWORKS** (previously unverified in v1.0/v1.1). The absence of a pass rule keeps the zone closed either way.

| Order | Action | Source | Destination |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Block and log IPv4 any | CYBER_QUARANTINE network | This Firewall |
| 2 | Block and log IPv4 any | CYBER_QUARANTINE network | RFC1918_NETWORKS (confirmed 22 Sep 2026) |
| Default | Implicit deny | Any | Any |

### 7.6 Why the gateway need not answer ping

A guest can use 10.10.x.1 as its default gateway even when ICMP echo and HTTPS to that address are blocked. Routing occurs at Layer 3 before permitted traffic is forwarded. For VM310, 10.10.10.1 was correct; inability to ping it was the expected effect of Block OPNsense Control Plane, not evidence of a wrong gateway.

### 8.4 HTTPS test interpretation

A raw TCP connection to 1.1.1.1:443 failed, but curl -4 completed an actual HTTPS transaction to Google with status 200. The HTTPS transaction is authoritative for public web access. A separate Google IPv4 variable test falsely failed because the command used NRR==1 instead of NR==1, leaving the variable empty. Future validation must use curl -4 and treat any non-000 HTTP status as proof that the public HTTPS service was reached.

### 8.5 Alias activation incident and correction

PROTECTED_NETWORKS was created and saved, but the alias change was initially left unapplied while dependent rules were edited. The GUI object existed, but Firewall → Diagnostics → Aliases showed an empty runtime table. The bottom Internet pass then matched traffic that should have been blocked, allowing VM310 to reach Proxmox. The operator identified the empty runtime table, applied the alias, confirmed all five entries, and repeated validation. Proxmox was then blocked while public HTTPS remained allowed.

**Required procedural control:** For a boundary alias, use Save → Apply on Aliases → verify the runtime table → edit or activate dependent rules → Apply firewall rules → clear only relevant states if required → validate positive and negative outcomes. **This same procedure was followed for the PROTECTED_NETWORKS content correction on 22 Sep 2026 (§7.3, §11.1 step 2, now closed): operator edited the two incorrect entries, saved, applied, and re-pulled the runtime table, which confirmed all five entries correct.**

## 9 Recovery points and rollback posture

**NAS backup boundary (clarified 22 Sep 2026):** nas01 is connected only to the cyberrange Proxmox host itself, for the host's own PVE configuration archive. It does not back up, mount into, or otherwise touch any guest VM/LXC internal to the cyberrange — including VM300 and VM301, even though both are non-disposable, protected infrastructure. Protection for every cyberrange guest is local Proxmox snapshots only. The one external-backup exception is cyber-fw01's OPNsense XML configuration export, retained separately via Admin01/Kali01 — not via NAS, and not a full VM backup. The "NAS vzdump"/"NAS backups" entries below predate this clarification and are retained as historical record only; do not repeat that pattern going forward.

| GTX 970's role (second GPU) | **Resolved 7 Oct 2026 (§3.3.6).** The host console was moved to the RX 7700 XT (the board POSTs on slot 1 and has no primary-GPU selector; AI-on-console-GPU validated safe), which **frees the GTX 970**. It is reassigned to its original intended role — non-AI GPU workload via exclusive vfio passthrough — now tracked as the build item below. |
| GTX 970 → hashcat vfio passthrough | **RESOLVED 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 1).** GTX 970 (`0f:00.0` VGA `10de:13c2` + `0f:00.1` HDMI-audio `10de:0fbb`) passed through to **VM310/Kali02** via vfio and validated at **11.6 GH/s hashcat (NTLM)**. Chain: (a) host IOMMU was off — enabled `iommu=pt` in `GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX_DEFAULT` (NB: `amd_iommu=on` is **invalid** on this AMD host, logs a cosmetic "AMD-Vi: Unknown option - 'on'"; `iommu=pt` + BIOS alone is correct) **and** turned on SVM/IOMMU in the X570 BIOS, which was the real blocker → 34 IOMMU groups, GTX 970 alone in group 28 (clean/isolated); (b) bound both functions to `vfio-pci` (`/etc/modprobe.d/vfio.conf`: `options vfio-pci ids=10de:13c2,10de:0fbb` + softdep pre: vfio-pci for nouveau/nvidiafb/snd_hda_intel; `blacklist-gpu-passthrough.conf` blacklists nouveau/nvidiafb) + `update-initramfs` (the "grub-efi-amd64 not installed / skipping ESP sync" warnings are cosmetic on this GRUB-booted host); (c) `qm set 310 -hostpci0 0000:0f:00,pcie=1`; (d) in Kali: `nvidia-driver` (550.163.01, supports Maxwell) — **not** `nvidia-cuda-toolkit` (no candidate, and not needed; driver provides libcuda) — then `linux-headers-$(uname -r)` + `dkms autoinstall` (DKMS was stuck "added" not "installed" with no headers → nvidia-smi "couldn't communicate"), and for hashcat `nvidia-opencl-icd ocl-icd-libopencl1` (no ICD was registered → "No OpenCL/CUDA platform"). **Lesson (render-node renumbering):** pulling the GTX 970's nouveau node shifted the RX 7700 XT render node `renderD129`→`renderD128`, breaking VM311/VM351 passthrough (`/dev/dri/renderD129 does not exist`) → fixed with `pct set 311/351 -dev0 /dev/dri/renderD128,gid=993`. See §3.3.6. |
| Dead GRUB kernel-parameter line | Low priority, not blocking. `/etc/default/grub` has a bare `video=efifb:off video=vesafb:off initcall_blacklist=sysfb_init` line that isn't inside `GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX_DEFAULT`/`GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX`, so it has no effect (confirmed via `/proc/cmdline`) — turned out to be unnecessary once the real fix (HDMI cable on the correct card) was in place. Harmless as-is; cleanup optional. See §3.3.5. |
| `7.0.14-14-pve` kernel package still installed | Low priority. The broken kernel from the §3.4.4 incident is still present and listed under "automatically selected" in `proxmox-boot-tool kernel list`, but is neutralized by the `7.0.14-19-pve` pin. `proxmox-boot-tool kernel remove` doesn't apply to auto-detected (non-manual) kernels; actual removal would need a direct package purge (exact package name not yet identified — `dpkg -l | grep pve-kernel` came up empty, this Proxmox version apparently uses different package naming). Not a live risk with the pin in place. |
| Linux process auditing (`auditd`/execve) — the Sysmon analog (deferred closeout) | **RESOLVED 10 Oct 2026 — auditd + execve rules + Wazuh audit localfile deployed on all monitored Linux boxes and baked into their `monitored-baseline` snapshots; Caldera Discovery confirmed surfacing on VM332.** (Original: added 9 Oct 2026 — surfaced running SOP-1.) Caldera Discovery abilities (`whoami`/`who`/`ps`) generated **no Wazuh alerts** because Linux doesn't log command execution by default — not a misconfig, a telemetry-source gap. Fix: install `auditd` + execve audit rules (`-S execve -k exec`, b64+b32) on the monitored Linux targets and add a Wazuh `<localfile>` (format `audit`, `/var/log/audit/audit.log`); then execve TTPs surface (audit rule group, ~80792). The Linux counterpart to the Windows Sysmon item. Should be baked into each monitored Linux box's baseline. |
| Re-baseline monitored boxes to a post-agent `monitored-baseline` snapshot (finding + action) | **RESOLVED 10 Oct 2026 — all ten monitored boxes (VLAN 20/30/40/70/71) now carry a `monitored-baseline` snapshot (Wazuh agent + auditd/Sysmon); that is the SOP revert target, not the old pre-agent `hardened_baseline`.** (Original: caught 9 Oct 2026 running SOP-1.) Every box's `hardened_baseline` snapshot predates the Wazuh agent (installed during 8H.2) and auditd, so a SOP Phase-G `qm rollback` to `hardened_baseline` would **strip the monitoring off the box**. Action: once a monitored box has its Wazuh agent (+ auditd on Linux / Sysmon on Windows), take a new **`monitored-baseline`** snapshot (on top of any deliberately-vulnerable state) and make *that* the SOP revert target. Applies to all monitored targets (VLAN 20/30/40/70/71). |
| Sysmon on the Windows zone (deferred closeout) | **RESOLVED 10 Oct 2026 — Sysmon (SwiftOnSecurity config) + Wazuh Sysmon-channel localfile deployed on all Windows boxes (DC01, VM321, VM322, VM371) and baked into their `monitored-baseline` snapshots.** (Original: install **Sysmon** on the VLAN 20 Windows hosts (DC01, VM321/cyber-ws01, VM322/cyber-ws02) with a maintained config (SwiftOnSecurity's or Olaf Hartong's `sysmon-modular`), and point the Wazuh agent at the Sysmon channel (add a `<localfile>` of location `Microsoft-Windows-Sysmon/Operational`, format `eventchannel`, to each agent's `ossec.conf`, then restart WazuhSvc). Native Windows logs alone miss process-level activity; Sysmon adds process-creation/network/image-load telemetry that maps to ATT&CK and makes the Kerberoast/BloodHound detection far richer. Recommended before serious Windows-side detection exercises; not a blocker for 8H.2. |
| Wazuh Docker-listener on VM340 (deferred closeout) | **RESOLVED 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 5).** Both layers deployed on VM340 `cyber-apps01` and web attacks confirmed surfacing. (a) **docker-listener wodle** added to `/var/ossec/etc/ossec.conf` for container lifecycle events (needs `docker` in the agent's Python). (b) **Web-log ingestion:** bind-mounted DVWA's apache logs to the host (`- /var/log/dvwa:/var/log/apache2` on the dvwa service in `/home/cyberadmin/apps/docker-compose.yml`) + a `<localfile>` (format `apache`, `/var/log/dvwa/access.log`) → **nikto/web attacks detected** (rules 31101/31151/sql_injection in Threat Hunting, src `cyber-apps01`). **Caveat:** container requests behind the Docker bridge/NAT show srcip `172.18.0.1` (the gateway), not the real attacker IP, in the raw access log — acceptable for detection; note it when writing srcip-based rules. Juice Shop's logs can be mounted the same way if/when needed. |
| 96-hour auto-revert of disposable targets | **BUILT + DRY-RUN VALIDATED 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 6); arm with the enable command below.** Implemented as `/usr/local/sbin/cyberrange-autorevert.sh` on the **Proxmox host** (guests can't self-revert), driven by a systemd `cyberrange-autorevert.service` (oneshot) + `.timer` (`OnBootSec=96h`, `OnUnitActiveSec=96h`, `Persistent=true`). The script carries a per-VM snapshot `MAP` (330–335, 340, 370, 371 → each box's clean baseline), **guards snapshot existence**, stops any running mapped VM, `qm rollback`s, and leaves it stopped; logs to `/var/log/cyberrange-autorevert.log`; supports `DRYRUN=1`. **Scope decision (operator): all disposable targets EXCEPT the AD zone (VLAN 20)** — DC01/VM321/VM322 are excluded so a forgotten AD scenario isn't nuked mid-exercise; cyber-fw01/kali01/kali02/VM350/VM351/VM360 are never in the map. Dry-run came back clean (all 9 mapped VMs matched a snapshot). **To arm:** `systemctl daemon-reload; systemctl enable --now cyberrange-autorevert.timer; systemctl list-timers cyberrange-autorevert.timer`. **Note:** the snapshot names in the map are inconsistent across boxes (`moniter-baseline`/`monitor-baseline`/`monitored-baseline`) — the script matches each exactly, but standardizing the names is a low-priority cleanup item. |
| DHCP static reservations for VM350 + VM360 + VM351 | **RESOLVED 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 2).** Static DHCP reservations created in OPNsense for all three — VM350 (`10.10.50.149`), VM351 (`10.10.50.144`), VM360 (`10.10.60.141`) — each confirmed showing its pinned address. The firewall rules / agent configs / AI pipeline / reissued indexer cert that hard-code these addresses can no longer drift under them. |
| defense-ai (VM351) false-positive prompt tuning | **Open, added 11 Oct 2026 — refinement, not blocking (§11.3.1k).** `deepseek-r1` over-calls FPs (flagged a benign Docker-overlayfs rootcheck anomaly as a "Critical rootkit"). Improve the poller's SOC-analyst prompt with known-FP context (Docker overlayfs `zoneinfo`/readdir patterns, expected BAS-tool artifacts) so automated severities are less noisy. Edit `/opt/ai-pipeline/poller.py` `SYS` prompt; no infra change. |
| ai-analysis Wazuh dashboard (VM351 pipeline) | **Open, added 11 Oct 2026 — optional polish (§11.3.1k).** The `ai-analysis-*` index pattern + Discover view is built/usable from VM360. A saved dashboard (severity breakdown + recent-analyses table) would be nicer than Discover — build when desired. |
| AI appliance HTTP mgmt path (Open-WebUI) | **Accepted, 11 Oct 2026 (§11.3.1k).** Open-WebUI is served HTTP (not TLS) on the internal VM360→VM351:8080 mgmt hop — acceptable for the lab; a reverse-proxy TLS front is an optional later add, not a rebuild. Also flip `ENABLE_SIGNUP=false` after the admin account exists. |
| `ai-pipeline` indexer credential | **Resolved 11 Oct 2026.** Rotated after being on-screen during Phase-7 troubleshooting; same scope (read `wazuh-alerts-*`, crud `ai-analysis-*`), `config.env` updated, pipeline re-verified. Broader Wazuh default-credential rotation (`admin`/`kibanaserver`/`wazuh-wui` via `wazuh-passwords-tool.sh`) remains an optional hardening item for the long-lived SIEM box. |
| VM311 agentic offensive-AI lane (Open-WebUI → Kali02 toolkit) | **RESOLVED 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 3).** Open-WebUI on VM311 (Attack-AI) now lets the model drive **Kali02's** offensive tooling over a scoped SSH bridge. Design (operator choices): **curated tool wrappers + a general shell tool**, running as a **dedicated `aiagent` user with scoped sudo** on Kali02 — not root, not the interactive user. Pieces: (a) an Open-WebUI **Tool** "Kali02 Offensive Toolkit" (Python/paramiko, `requirements: paramiko` auto-pip) with Valves (`KALI_HOST=10.10.10.177`, `KALI_USER=aiagent`, `KEY_PATH=/opt/ai-agent/aiagent_key` ed25519, `CMD_TIMEOUT=600`, `MAX_CHARS=9000`) and methods `run_nmap/run_gobuster_dir/run_ffuf/run_dirb/run_nikto/run_whatweb/run_enum4linux/run_sqlmap/run_hydra/run_hashcat/run_john/run_command`; (b) Kali02 `/etc/sudoers.d/aiagent` scoping NOPASSWD to a raw-socket tool allowlist (`AI_PENTEST = nmap, masscan, arp-scan, tcpdump, hping3, nping`). **Critical finding:** the model will **narrate** tool use without calling the tool unless Open-WebUI function-calling is set to **Native** (not Default) — with **Native + `qwen3-abliterated:14b`** the tool fired and correctly identified DVWA. Default mode (tried with hermes3 and qwen3) never actually invoked it. Constraint preserved: nothing opens toward kali01; this lane is Attack-AI (VM311) → Kali02 only. |
| GPU coil whine on RX 7700 XT (inference) | **CLOSED — diagnosed, not software-fixable 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 4).** The whine is **inference coil whine** — per-token current pulsing as the GPU returns a response (reproducible: it starts exactly when a response begins and stops when cancelled; `echo high` to the perf level reproduces it). **Not** fixable via OverDrive: the operator swept fixed clocks (2300/2200/2000/1800/1600/1400/1200) and undervolt down to −125 mV with **no change in pitch or level**. Root cause is PSU/VRM-side electrical, not voltage/frequency. OverDrive clocks were reverted to baseline (`s 0 500` / `s 1 2300` / `vo -50`; the PCI-anchored `gpu-undervolt.service` remains for the thermals it does help). **Resolution path is hardware** — a better/quieter PSU and physical dampening — tracked as an operator hardware-investment item, not an open software task. See §11.3.1k. |
| Wazuh API connection down after VM350 reboots (recurring) | **RESOLVED 14 Oct 2026 — verified across a clean cold reboot.** Root cause was a **cold-boot start-time race**, not memory (confirmed: indexer heap only `-Xmx1024m`, 12 GiB free, 0 swap used, no OOM; `systemd-analyze blame` showed `wazuh-indexer` taking **68 s** to start while docker/containerd/snapd churned, starving `wazuh-manager`'s `wazuh-control start` so systemd TERM'd it mid-startup — on other boots the indexer itself timed out). Fix = **stagger the three heavy services + raise their start timeouts** so a slow-but-successful start isn't killed: (a) `/usr/local/sbin/wait-wazuh-backends.sh` (dashboard ExecStartPre; waits for indexer :9200 + manager API :55000, best-effort `exit 0` on timeout); (b) `/usr/local/sbin/wait-wazuh-indexer.sh` (manager ExecStartPre; best-effort wait for :9200 to stagger manager behind the indexer burst); (c) `systemctl edit wazuh-indexer` → `TimeoutStartSec=600`; (d) `systemctl edit wazuh-manager` → `After=wazuh-indexer.service network-online.target`, `TimeoutStartSec=600`, the wait-indexer ExecStartPre; (e) `wazuh-dashboard` drop-in with `After=wazuh-indexer wazuh-manager` + the wait-backends ExecStartPre. `After=` (not `Requires=`) so an indexer failure never cascades. No `jvm.options` change and no BAS-autostart deferral were needed. Cold reboot now comes up API-connected with no manual restart. |
| ART (Atomic Red Team) OPNsense rules not yet built in OPNsense | **Catalog documented 13 Oct 2026 (deferred item 7); rules still to be created when running atomics.** The ART firewall catalog (5 pre-built-disabled rules `ART-WINDOWS`/`-LINUX`/`-APPS`/`-HYBRID`/`-HYBRID-INT`, VM350→target on CYBER_SIEM, WinRM/SSH per zone) and full operation SOP-6 are written in `claude/SOP-BAS-Scenarios.md` (§0.3 + SOP-6). **They are documented, not yet created in OPNsense.** When you start running atomics: build the 5 rules (disabled), enable target remoting + open the target's own host firewall to VM350, stage runner creds, take the `monitored-baseline`, then enable→run→observe→cleanup→disable→revert per SOP-6. |

### 11.2 Gate 8G2 — complete the attack side (VM310 Kali02, VM311 attack-ai, VM312 Parrot01) — CLOSED 26 Sep 2026

**11.2.0 — BlackArch abandoned; VM310 rebuilt as Kali02. Major scope revision, 23 Sep 2026.**

- **What happened:** Gate 8G2 step 2 (below, original numbering) chased a QEMU Guest Agent install on the original VM310 BlackArch build through a long diagnostic chain: a corrupted/unintentionally-tmpfs-mounted GnuPG keyring, a dead legacy keyserver (`pool.sks-keyservers.net`) hardcoded in both `dirmngr.conf` and `gpg.conf`, an under-trusted packager key resolved via an `archlinux-keyring`/`blackarch-keyring` update, a `libgcc`/`gcc-libs` file-conflict (Arch package split, resolved via `pacman -S qemu-guest-agent --overwrite`), then — once qemu-guest-agent's *files* were installed — a **GLIBC_2.38 not found** runtime failure, proving the underlying system was too far out of date for the package to actually run. Chasing that opened a full `pacman -Syu` attempt, which hit a real, known upstream packaging bug (Arch [FS#79890](https://bugs.archlinux.org/task/79890): `jdk-openjdk`/`jre-openjdk` merge conflict, fixed upstream Nov 2023 but this VM predated the fix), then a second file-conflict (`libstdc++` vs `gcc-libs`, same split pattern as `libgcc`), then a *third* blocker (`jdk11-openjdk`/`jre11-openjdk`, same merge pattern) plus an unrelated `python-typing_extensions`/`python-uvicorn` dependency break. Every failed attempt aborted cleanly at pacman's "prepare transaction" stage — nothing was ever left partially applied.
- **Operator decision, 23 Sep 2026: stop fighting it.** Research confirmed BlackArch has not shipped a new full/slim ISO since **2023.04.01/2023.05.01** — the same vintage already installed on VM310 (installed May 2023) — so even a from-scratch reinstall from the current official ISO would not have avoided the same eventual first-time `pacman -Syu` gauntlet. Given BlackArch was only being evaluated out of curiosity against Kali (the operator's established daily driver) and there is no meaningful project-vision cost to dropping it, the operator chose to **abandon BlackArch entirely** rather than continue excavating years of accumulated package-split/rename debt on a single disposable attack box.
- **Revised attack-side plan (operator-approved, expands original two-attack-host design to three):**
  - **VM310 → renamed `Kali02`, wiped, rebuilt as Kali Linux 2026.2.** (Not `Kali01` — that identity is already VM301/Admin01; keeping VM310 as `Kali02` preserves the same collision-avoidance intent the original naming convention was built around.)
  - **VM311 → attack-ai** (LXC), unchanged from the original §3.3.2 plan — no VMID conflict.
  - **VM312 → Parrot01** (Parrot OS), a new third attack host. VM312 was chosen specifically so it does not collide with VM311's already-planned attack-ai reservation.
  - Sequencing (operator-specified): finish Kali02 baseline → build attack-ai LXC next (slim baseline only — full model buildout is blocked on the pending AI GPU hardware upgrade, per §3.3.3) → then build Parrot01. Once all three are done, the attack side is considered complete (with attack-ai's full buildout explicitly understood as hardware-dependent, not a blocking gap), and the project moves to the next zone.
- **ISO sourcing:** current Kali release researched (`kali-linux-2026.2-installer-amd64.iso`, https://cdimage.kali.org/kali-2026.2/). Operator downloaded it directly from kali.org, verified its SHA256 against the live download page, and uploaded it to the `ISO-Repo` NAS share via the NAS's own GUI — deliberately **not** from the cyberrange Proxmox host, since that host's `NAS-ISO-Repo-CyberRange` mount uses the `polaris-cyberrange-iso-ro` credential, which is **read-only by design** (§3.4.2). The old BlackArch ISO was removed from the share via the same NAS-GUI route. The cyberrange node's existing read-only mount picked up the new file with no changes needed on that side.
- **Rebuild executed:** VM310's existing virtual disk was wiped via the Kali installer's guided full-disk partitioning (existing BlackArch-era snapshot remains as a rollback point if ever needed — not used). Proxmox display name changed `Blackarchlinux01` → `Kali02` (Options tab). Result: Kali02 installed, guest hostname `kali-attack`, DHCP-assigned 10.10.10.177 (same address as before), `ping`/public reachability confirmed. **`apt update && apt upgrade` completed cleanly on first try — no mirror-list issue**, unlike the Arch/BlackArch ordeal above. `qemu-guest-agent` was already present from the ISO; verified **active/running** guest-side (`systemctl status`) and **reachable** Proxmox-side (`qm agent 310 ping` returns clean; Options tab confirms QEMU Guest Agent = Enabled) with no additional VM restart needed, since the Proxmox-side `agent: 1` setting predated the OS reinstall. **QEMU Guest Agent item is now fully CLOSED for Kali02** — the entire original goal of Gate 8G2 step 2 (below) is achieved, just via a different OS than originally planned.
- **Historical BlackArch-era findings below (11.2.1, step 2) are retained for the record but are now moot** — that guest OS no longer exists on VM310. Steps 1, 3, and 4 (naming convention, DHCP, no-preload-tooling policy) were architecture/policy decisions independent of the guest OS and carry forward unchanged to Kali02.

**11.2.1 — original steps (BlackArch-era; superseded above, kept for history)**

1. ~~Confirm and document the final Proxmox display name and guest hostname~~ **CLOSED 22 Sep 2026 — not a reconciliation item; this is the intended naming convention.** Operator clarified: the Proxmox-side display name and the guest's internal hostname are deliberately **not** meant to match. Two separate naming layers:
   - **Proxmox display name — deterministic, structured.** Based on host type/distro plus a sequence number scoped to what's on that VLAN, giving the operator predictable, controlled internal naming.
   - **Guest-internal hostname — unstructured, decided ad hoc at build time.** No naming scheme; picked as each box is built. The point is specifically to *avoid* the guest hostname echoing the Proxmox name or anything resembling the admin box's identity.
   - VM310 (then BlackArch) matched this pattern as-is: Proxmox name `Blackarchlinux01`, guest shell identity `acinomed-blackarch`. **Superseded 23 Sep 2026:** VM310 is now `Kali02` / `kali-attack` — same convention, new values (§11.2.0).

Install offensive tooling only after the baseline recovery point exists and use it only against explicitly selected cyberrange targets.~~ **AGREED 22 Sep 2026, with an added policy clarification:** offensive tooling is intentionally **not** pre-loaded as part of the build. Operator's stated reasoning: the universe of possible offensive tools is too large to usefully pre-stage, and disk space is conserved by installing tools only as actually needed for a given exercise, not speculatively. **Still in effect on Kali02** and is the general policy for Parrot01 (VM312) as well.

**11.3.1 Windows AD zone scope, and other open build-catalog items (26 Sep 2026)**

- **Windows AD lab scope finalized:** the roadmap's original single "VM320 Windows target" placeholder was a connectivity-verification stub, not the intended final scope. Operator wants a real small Active Directory lab to start: **VM320 domain controller (Windows Server 2025 evaluation)**, plus **VM321 (Windows 11)** and **VM322 (Windows 10)** as two additional domain-joined boxes — a realistic mixed-OS AD environment. Sequencing: build and validate VM320 first, then domain-join VM321/VM322. Exact roles for VM321/VM322 beyond "domain-joined Windows box" (pure workstation vs. server-adjacent role) not yet decided.
- **Build philosophy confirmed, consistent with the rest of the project:** clean baseline install first → snapshot → identify and harden whatever default-install/default-config weaknesses are found (an industry-standard hardening pass, not scripted-in vulnerabilities) → snapshot the hardened baseline → deliberate vulnerabilities introduced later as an explicit, separate step for practice scenarios. Same clean-then-harden-then-break pattern as the rest of the range.
- **Purposefully vulnerable hosts (Vulnhub, Metasploitable3, DVWA, OWASP Juice Shop, etc.) — explicitly open-ended.** Operator wants these incorporated wherever they best fit topically (confirmed: DVWA and Juice Shop belong in the Apps zone, VM340) but does not want a fixed catalog decided now — described as "an open thought/discussion part of the build." Treat this as a running list to propose additions to as each zone is actually built, not a backlog to fill in all at once.
- **SIEM zone breach-and-attack-simulation tool — resolved 26 Sep 2026.** Operator confirmed **Infection Monkey** (Akamai/Guardicore) as the specific tool originally intended. All three candidates presented are approved for VM350: **Infection Monkey** (primary), **MITRE Caldera**, and **Atomic Red Team** — all three to be incorporated in the SIEM zone build.
- **Design intent behind the AD zone — explicitly captured, 27 Sep 2026:** operator's purpose for this zone is BloodHound-driven AD attack-path practice — Kerberoasting/AS-REP roasting, TGT/ticket attacks (pass-the-ticket, golden/silver ticket), and privilege-escalation chains through service accounts, admin sessions, and group-membership abuse. **Assessed and confirmed sufficient as scoped:** the 3-box plan (1 DC + 2 domain-joined clients) is architecturally adequate for all of the above — these techniques don't require more machines, they require *content*. What will actually make the zone useful for this purpose is deferred to the later deliberate-misconfiguration phase (not today's clean-baseline build): populating AD with realistic objects (nested group memberships, service accounts with SPNs for Kerberoasting, an account or two with Kerberos pre-auth disabled for AS-REP roasting, a messy ACL or two, cached admin sessions on a workstation). For this zone specifically, "introduce vulnerabilities later" means AD-object-level misconfiguration, not an unpatched CVE or a vulnerable app — a different flavor of "vulnerable" than the Linux/app zones.
  - **Confirmed future expansion (not in current scope):** cross-domain/cross-forest trust abuse practice. Would require a second domain — explicitly intended by the operator as a later expansion, not part of the initial VM320-322 build.
  - **Tooling placement confirmed:** BloodHound, a collector (SharpHound or bloodhound-python), and Kerberos attack tooling (Rubeus, Impacket, certipy, etc.) will live on the attack side (Kali02), not on the DC. Operator's stated plan: begin building out this tooling once the AD structure itself is solid — not blocking today's build, a follow-on step once VM320-322 exist and the domain has real content to collect against.

These recommendations do not authorize downloading, installing, or exposing a vulnerable workload. The exact OS edition and application/SIEM selection remain approval-gate decisions.

**11.3.1b Remaining-zone build-out roadmap, agreed 2 Oct 2026**

With the Windows AD zone (VM320-322) fully built, hardened, and content-populated (§11.3.3), operator set the order for the rest of the build. Explicit operator rationale: finish all remaining zones first, then return to Gate 8H's internal-firewall design, since the rest of the build may itself inform how the internal firewall needs to be managed/configured — i.e. don't design the firewall in a vacuum before there's real cross-zone traffic and tooling to design it around.

**Agreed order:**
1. **Linux defense zone (VM330, VLAN 30)** — a few Linux servers plus Metasploitable, per §11.3's existing table entry. **COMPLETE 3 Oct 2026** — all six hosts (VM330-335) built, validated, and (where applicable) hardened/vulnerability-injected.
2. **Apps zone (VM340, VLAN 40)** — DVWA and Juice Shop (already confirmed), plus Vulnhub-style boxes. **COMPLETE 4 Oct 2026** — DVWA and Juice Shop built as Docker containers on a hardened Ubuntu host; Vulnhub-style boxes left as future optional additions to this zone, not blocking.
3. **Windows/Linux combination zone (VM370-379, new VLAN 70/CYBER_HYBRID)** — a **new zone, not in the original six-zone Gate 6 plan.** Operator confirmed this is wanted as its own distinct zone (not folded into the existing Windows or Linux zones) specifically to preserve clean separation between pure-Windows, pure-Linux, and mixed-trust scenarios. **VLAN 70 infrastructure COMPLETE 4 Oct 2026** — see §11.3.1c. Operator then specified a detailed capstone scenario for this zone (SQLi entry → hash crack → chisel-tunneled lateral movement → AD privesc) rather than a simple single-host target, bringing a second new VLAN (71/CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL, dual-homed pivot design) into scope — see §11.3.1d for the design and §11.3.1e for its **also-COMPLETE 4 Oct 2026** infrastructure build. See §5.1/§5.2/§5.4 for addressing (`10.10.70.0/24` and `10.10.71.0/24`, VMIDs 370-379) and §11 table rows. VM370 (`cyber-dmz01`) OS build through `hardened-baseline` snapshot is **CLOSED (4 Oct 2026)** — see §11.3.1f, which also documents a significant Dnsmasq static-DNS defect found and worked around (fix: Unbound Host Overrides, §5.3). **VM370's web layer build is also CLOSED (5 Oct 2026)** — see §11.3.1g, which documents the Docker/nginx stack, a MySQL `x86-64-v2` platform fix, a second real networking defect found and fixed (missing static IP on `ens18`), and the first full positive validation of the CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID attack path. **VM371 (`cyber-corpsrv01`) is also CLOSED (7 Oct 2026, operator-confirmed — see §11.3.1h): Windows Server 2022, single-homed on VLAN 71, domain-joined to the existing forest, `svc_legacysync` created with WinRM + `SeImpersonatePrivilege`, full chisel-relayed pivot validated. This zone (item 3) is therefore fully closed.** Active step: SIEM zone (item 5 below), VM350/351 — the Analysis zone (item 4) closed 7 Oct 2026 (§11.3.1i).
4. **Analysis zone content (VM360, VLAN 60)** — the VM has existed since the original connectivity-validation build but currently carries **no tooling at all**; this step actually populates it. MITRE ATT&CK Navigator is the confirmed first piece — a reference/mapping tool for tracking technique coverage, kept deliberately separate from the active simulation tooling below. **CLOSED 7 Oct 2026 (§11.3.1i):** VM360 rebuilt from a headless validation probe into an Ubuntu Desktop 26.04 analyst workstation (DHCP `10.10.60.141`), with Navigator running as a loopback-only Docker container viewed on-box — no cross-zone exposure. Next: item 5, SIEM zone.
5. **SIEM zone (VM350/351, VLAN 50)** — Wazuh, defense-ai (**deepseek-r1 14B**, operator's starting model choice), and the three breach-and-attack-simulation tools already confirmed 26 Sep 2026 (Infection Monkey, MITRE Caldera, Atomic Red Team — see §11.3.1). Deliberately sequenced after the other content zones exist, so the SIEM has real cross-zone traffic and attack activity to detect from day one rather than being tuned against nothing. **CORE CLOSED 8 Oct 2026 (§11.3.1j):** VM350 `cyber-siem01` built with Wazuh (native all-in-one), Infection Monkey (Docker), Caldera 5.3.0 (native service), and the ART runner — all persistent, serving locally, nothing cross-zone yet. **VM351 defense-ai now also CLOSED 11 Oct 2026 (§11.3.1k)** — `cyber-defense-ai01` GPU AI appliance (Ollama/ROCm with deepseek-r1:14b + llama-guard3:8b, Open-WebUI, and a Wazuh→AI alert-triage pipeline writing an `ai-analysis-*` index). SIEM zone (item 5) is therefore fully closed; Gate 8H completed earlier. Next: Gate 9 (fail-closed exercise + closeout) and the remaining §10 deferred items (GTX 970 hashcat passthrough, DHCP reservations, Docker-listener, 96h auto-revert).
6. **Gate 8H** — internal/scenario firewall design (§11.5/§11.5.1). Infection Monkey and Caldera's need to reach across zones to simulate lateral movement is noted as a concrete, specific use case to design Gate 8H's scoped cross-zone passes around, rather than a hypothetical one.
7. **C2 framework addition to the Attack zone (VLAN 10), explicitly last.** Operator wants to add a C2 framework (Sliver and/or Covenant under consideration) to the existing attack hosts, specifically to learn C2 infrastructure and usage — intentionally sequenced after everything else so it gets dedicated, unhurried attention rather than being squeezed in alongside other zone build-out work.

**Additional candidate content ideas discussed and accepted** for zones 1-3 above are logged in §11.3's "Purposefully vulnerable hosts" block, as optional starting points, not a fixed catalog — exact content for each zone still gets finalized when that zone is actually built, consistent with the project's established pattern.

**11.3.1c VLAN 70 (CYBER_HYBRID) infrastructure build — execution log (4 Oct 2026)**

With the Linux and Apps zones both closed, built the required new-zone infrastructure for the Windows/Linux combination zone before any guest, per item 3's requirements above.

1. **Proxmox trunk update (VM300):** `net1`'s trunk list (`qm config 300 | grep net1`) confirmed as `trunks=10;20;30;40;50;60;99` — tag 70 genuinely absent, confirming the same root cause as the original VLAN 60/Gate 8E DHCP break would otherwise have recurred here. Updated via `qm set 300 --net1 "virtio=BC:24:11:4A:4F:C9,bridge=vmbr1,firewall=1,trunks=10;20;30;40;50;60;70;99"`. **Real debugging history, same bug class as VM332's unquoted-boot-order slip:** the first attempt was run unquoted, and bash split the comma-separated value on each `;`, treating `20`, `30`, `40`, etc. as separate (failed) commands — only `trunks=10` actually landed. Diagnosed immediately from the `-bash: 20: command not found`-style errors, fixed by quoting the entire `--net1` value, and reverified via `qm config 300 | grep net1` showing the full `trunks=10;20;30;40;50;60;70;99` list.
2. **OPNsense VLAN interface:** created under Interfaces → Other Types → VLAN (parent `vtnet1`, tag 70, description `CYBER_HYBRID`), assigned as interface `opt8`/`vlan08`, enabled with static IPv4 `10.10.70.1/24`, no gateway — same pattern as the other seven zones. Came up active immediately in the Assignments list alongside all seven existing zones.
3. **Trunk change confirmed live, no cyber-fw01 reboot needed:** the new interface showed green/active status as soon as it was created, meaning the Proxmox-side trunk update from step 1 took effect on the running VM without requiring a restart — a real open question going in (flagged explicitly beforehand given cyber-fw01's design as the range's single point of failure), now answered empirically for the record.
4. **DHCP (Dnsmasq):** `CYBER_HYBRID` added to Dnsmasq's interface list, range `10.10.70.100-10.10.70.199` — ninth range alongside LAN and the other seven cyber zones.
5. **DNS split (Unbound → Dnsmasq):** two entries added under Unbound's **Query Forwarding** tab (not "Domain Overrides" — correcting §5.3's earlier loose terminology for this same mechanism): `hybrid.cyberrange.home.arpa` → `127.0.0.1#53053`, and the reverse zone `70.10.10.in-addr.arpa` → `127.0.0.1#53053`. Confirmed matching the existing seven zones' entries exactly (17 total Query Forwarding entries post-change: 7 zones × 2 + `polaris.home.arpa` + the 2 new).
6. **Firewall — standard four-rule active-purpose policy** applied to the new `CYBER_HYBRID` interface itself: DNS pass (order 1), control-plane block (order 2), `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block (order 3), Internet pass (order 4) — confirmed via GUI screenshot, same order as all other zones. **Minor cosmetic finding, not yet corrected:** the Internet-pass rule's description still reads "Allow Cyber_Analysis Internet," left over from being cloned from the CYBER_ANALYSIS zone's rule template — functionally correct, description just needs renaming next time this interface's rules are touched.
7. **Attack-path exception added proactively** (new §7.4.4) as part of this same infrastructure build, continuing the pattern established in §7.4.1-§7.4.3 — not discovered missing after VM370 is built.
8. **No `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` alias change needed** — `10.10.70.0/24` already falls inside the alias's existing `10.0.0.0/8` entry, same as every other zone.

**VLAN 70 (CYBER_HYBRID) infrastructure CLOSED 4 Oct 2026.** Full positive zone-segmentation validation (the standard §12.1 block) is deferred until a guest exists in VM370-379 — there is nothing to test reachability against yet. Next: build the first guest(s) in this zone.

**11.3.1d VM370/371 capstone scenario — design and architecture decisions (4 Oct 2026)**

With VLAN 70 (CYBER_HYBRID) infrastructure complete and no guest yet built, operator specified a detailed capstone scenario for this zone rather than a simple single-host target — explicitly described as intended to incorporate multiple tools and techniques together, closer to a final exam than another standalone box.

**Full scenario brief, as given:** a Linux box (VM370) hosts a public-facing web presence with a discoverable subdomain structure. The main site is a functional multi-page fake site (finance-themed candidate, not yet confirmed) with several clickable pages. A second subdomain hosts a vulnerable WordPress instance as a deliberate red herring — discoverable, exploitable, but leads nowhere. A third, "forgotten developer" subdomain is the real entry point: a custom app vulnerable to SQL injection, whose database holds hashed credentials for an account on the Windows box (VM371). VM371 sits on an internal-only network relative to VM370, requiring internal IP discovery and lateral movement (evil-winrm or xfreerdp3) once the hash is cracked. Once on VM371, Potato-family local privilege escalation plus BloodHound-driven path discovery leads to top-level (Domain Admin) access. Tooling intended to be exercised across the full chain: Burp Suite/Caido, gobuster/ffuf, Nmap, BloodHound, WinPEAS/LinPEAS, WordPress-specific tooling, a hash cracker, SQLi tooling, and a tunneling framework (chisel) to move tools from the Linux box onto the Windows box. Operator specifically floated this build as the place to introduce the internal/scenario firewall concept (Gate 8H) at a zone-local scale — a DMZ-style split with tunneling as the only path across it — ahead of Gate 8H's own formal range-wide design.

**Three architecture decisions confirmed via operator selection, 4 Oct 2026:**

1. **New VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL), with a dual-homed Linux pivot box**, rather than keeping VM371 on the existing VLAN 70 behind host-firewall rules alone. VM370 gets a second NIC on the new VLAN 71; VM371 lives solely on VLAN 71 with no other path in. This gives the scenario a real network-layer DMZ boundary (matching the operator's own stated interest in trying this at zone scale) rather than simulating one with iptables/Windows Defender Firewall rules on a single flat segment.
2. **VM371 joins the existing `corp.cyberrange.local` AD forest** (VM320 DC01), rather than standing up a second, standalone domain. "Top-level admin" for this scenario therefore means Domain Admin on the real, already-built forest — the same Kerberoastable (`cyber_svc`) / AS-REP-roastable (`jthompson`) / nested-group privilege-escalation chain already populated in the AD zone (§11.3.1) becomes the actual BloodHound target from this new entry point, rather than a second escalation chain built from scratch.
3. **Web layer built as Docker containers behind an nginx reverse proxy** on VM370, rather than a native LAMP install — consistent with the project's established Apps-zone precedent (VM340, §11.3.5) of keeping vulnerable/application workloads containerized and independently resettable while the host OS stays on the standard hardened-baseline pattern.

**Resulting host plan:**

- **VM370 (`cyber-dmz01`):** dual-homed Linux host. NIC1 on VLAN 70 (CYBER_HYBRID, DHCP). NIC2 statically addressed on VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL) at `10.10.71.10`. Standard hardened-baseline install pattern, then Docker + nginx reverse proxy hosting three subdomains under `hybrid.cyberrange.home.arpa`: a main site (content/branding TBD), a decoy WordPress blog, and a "forgotten dev" SQLi app seeding an NTLM hash for a VM371 account. Also runs the chisel server side, since VM370 is the only host with a leg in both networks.
- **VM371 (`cyber-corpsrv01`):** Windows host, single NIC on VLAN 71 only. Domain-joined to `corp.cyberrange.local`. No direct network path from CYBER_ATTACK or anywhere else except through VM370 — reachable only via chisel tunnel once VM370 is compromised.

**Explicitly decided: no CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL firewall rule, ever**, breaking the pattern established for every other target zone (§7.4.1-§7.4.4). The only legitimate way into VLAN 71 is VM370's own second NIC plus a chisel tunnel — this is the point of the scenario, not a gap to close. See §11.3.1e for the resulting infrastructure build and §7.4's closing note.

**Not yet decided, to be settled when that build step is reached:** the main site's theme/branding (candidate: a fake finance company), exact page content for the decoy WordPress and main site, and the precise narrative framing for the seeded NTLM hash (candidate: leftover debug data from a legacy sync job).

**Gate 8H scope note, for the record:** this zone-local dual-homed design is a scenario-specific segmentation decision, not a declaration that Gate 8H (the range-wide internal/scenario firewall design, §11.5/§11.5.1) is itself closed. Gate 8H remains open and still scheduled per the §11.3.1b roadmap, after all remaining zones are built.

**11.3.1e VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL) infrastructure build — execution log (4 Oct 2026)**

Per §11.3.1d's decision 1, built the new VLAN 71 infrastructure before either VM370 or VM371, same discipline as every other new zone in this project.

1. **Proxmox trunk update (VM300):** confirmed prior state `trunks=10;20;30;40;50;60;70;99` (tag 71 absent, as expected), updated via `qm set 300 --net1 "virtio=BC:24:11:4A:4F:C9,bridge=vmbr1,firewall=1,trunks=10;20;30;40;50;60;70;71;99"` — **quoted correctly on the first attempt this time**, having learned the lesson from the VLAN 70 build's live unquoted-semicolon failure (§11.3.1c) and VM332's earlier boot-order incident. No error, confirmed via `qm config 300 | grep net1` showing the full updated trunk list with tag 71 present.
2. **OPNsense VLAN interface:** created under Interfaces → Other Types → VLAN (parent `vtnet1`, tag 71, description `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL`), assigned as interface `opt9`/`vlan09`, enabled with static IPv4 `10.10.71.1/24`, no gateway — same pattern as all eight existing zones.
3. **DHCP (Dnsmasq):** range `10.10.71.100-10.10.71.199` added, domain `hybrid-internal.cyberrange.home.arpa` set on the DHCP range's own Domain field. **Operator caught and corrected a placement question mid-build:** confirmed by checking the General and Domains tabs directly that the per-range Domain field is correct — it assigns the zone's local-resolution domain, while the Domains tab is for forwarding overrides to another upstream resolver (not what's needed here) and General only controls global Dnsmasq behavior. **Operator also asked whether `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL` needed adding to Dnsmasq's General-tab listening interfaces given the zone's "internal" nature — confirmed yes, it does:** the Domain field and the listening-interfaces list are orthogonal; the zone's internal/no-gateway reachability posture doesn't exempt it from needing Dnsmasq to actually bind to and serve DNS/DHCP on that interface, and in fact makes it more necessary, since hosts on this zone have no other path to resolve names. Added `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL` to the General-tab listening interfaces list alongside the other eight, confirmed and applied.
4. **DNS split (Unbound → Dnsmasq):** two Query Forwarding entries added — `hybrid-internal.cyberrange.home.arpa` and the reverse zone `71.10.10.in-addr.arpa`, both → `127.0.0.1#53053`. Confirmed 19 total Query Forwarding entries post-change (9 zones × 2 + `polaris.home.arpa` + the 2 new).
5. **Firewall — standard four-rule active-purpose policy** applied to `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL` itself (DNS pass, control-plane block, `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block, Internet pass), same order as every other zone. Confirmed via GUI, applied.
6. **Firewall — narrow AD-ports-only pass, `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL → CYBER_WINDOWS`,** inserted between the control-plane block and the `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` block (same positioning logic as the CYBER_ATTACK exception template, §7.4.1-§7.4.4). Built as **two separate rules with two port aliases**, operator's explicit choice "in the interest of continuing the realistic production theme" rather than one combined TCP/UDP rule:
   - `AD_TCP_PORTS` (TCP): `53,88,135,389,445,464,636,3268` — all eight AD ports run over TCP.
   - `AD_UDP_Ports` (UDP): `53,88,389,464` — only DNS, Kerberos, LDAP, and kpasswd are genuinely dual-protocol; 135 (RPC endpoint mapper), 445 (SMB), 636 (LDAPS), and 3268 (Global Catalog LDAP) are TCP-only.
   - **Real build correction:** the first pass at the alias content split all eight ports across the two aliases with no overlap (`AD_TCP_PORTS` = `135,445,636,3268` only; `AD_UDP_Ports` = `53,88,389,464` only), which would have silently broken LDAP enumeration, Kerberos ticket exchange on larger tickets (PAC/group-membership data), and DNS zone-transfer/large-response behavior — all of which need the TCP path, not just the UDP one, and which matter directly for the BloodHound/Kerberoasting work this scenario is built around. Corrected by adding the four dual-protocol ports back into `AD_TCP_PORTS` so it carries the full eight, while `AD_UDP_Ports` correctly keeps only the four that are truly dual-protocol. Confirmed via alias listing post-fix, applied.
7. **No `PROTECTED_NETWORKS` alias change needed** — `10.10.71.0/24` already falls inside the alias's existing `10.0.0.0/8` entry, same as every other zone.
8. **No CYBER_ATTACK pass rule added, by design** — see §11.3.1d decision 1 and §7.4's closing note. This is the first target-adjacent zone in the project to deliberately not receive the §7.4.1-§7.4.4 exception template.

**VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL) infrastructure CLOSED 4 Oct 2026.** Full positive/negative validation (AD-ports reachability to CYBER_WINDOWS, confirmed absence of any CYBER_ATTACK path) is deferred until VM370/VM371 exist to generate real traffic — there is nothing to test against yet. Next: build VM370 (`cyber-dmz01`).

**11.3.1f VM370 (cyber-dmz01) build, and a significant Dnsmasq static-DNS defect found and worked around (4 Oct 2026)**

With VLAN 70/71 infrastructure complete, built the first guest in the capstone zone.

**VM370 Proxmox build and OS install:**

1. **Created via `qm create 370`:** `--name cyber-dmz01 --sockets 1 --cores 2 --memory 4096 --net0 virtio,bridge=vmbr1,tag=70,firewall=1 --net1 virtio,bridge=vmbr1,tag=71,firewall=1 --scsihw virtio-scsi-pci --ostype l26`, `--scsi0 Cyber_VM:40`, ISO from `NAS-ISO-Repo-CyberRange`, `--agent enabled=1`. Confirmed dual-NIC MACs via `qm config 370 | grep net`.
2. **Ubuntu 26.04 Server** installed via Subiquity — operator's explicit choice as "the typical distro for web-hosting servers," no reason found to use otherwise. `ens18` (VLAN 70, DHCP) briefly failed autoconfiguration during install while `ens19` (VLAN 71) succeeded; a manual retry in the installer pulled an address immediately. Assessed as installer timing, not a Dnsmasq listening-interfaces gap (CYBER_HYBRID was confirmed already present in that list) — logged as a minor non-blocking finding, not pursued further.
3. **Dual-NIC netplan:** `ens19` set static (`10.10.71.10/24`, `dhcp4: false`), `ens18` left on DHCP (`dhcp4: true`). Confirmed via `ip -4 addr show`. **Real correction:** Ubuntu 26.04 uses `systemd-networkd`, not the legacy ISC `dhclient` (command not found) — used `networkctl renew ens18` / `systemctl restart systemd-networkd` instead.
4. **Hardening pass**, same pattern as prior Linux-zone hosts, run as a batched sequence per operator's standing preference for mechanical/repetitive work: `apt update && apt full-upgrade`, SSH root login disabled, UFW default-deny-incoming/allow-outgoing with SSH **deliberately scoped to CYBER_ATTACK (`10.10.10.0/24`) only** rather than this project's usual blanket-SSH-allow (flagged to operator, not objected to), ports 80/443 opened, UFW enabled, unattended-upgrades installed/enabled, fail2ban installed/enabled with the `sshd` jail active. Confirmed via `ufw status verbose` and `fail2ban-client status`.
5. **Snapshotted as `hardened-baseline`** (`qm snapshot 370 hardened-baseline`), description noting dual-homed addressing and the hardening state, pre-Docker. Confirmed present in the Snapshots tab.

**Capstone branding decided:** main site theme is **Meridian Capital Partners** (fake finance company, operator's selection), decoy WordPress blog is **Meridian Insights** — settling the "not yet decided" item left open in §11.3.1d.

**DNS troubleshooting saga — root cause, elimination process, and fix:**

Attempting to register `cyber-dmz01.hybrid.cyberrange.home.arpa` plus three subdomain aliases (`www`, `blog`, `portal-dev`) via a Dnsmasq Host Override (Services → Dnsmasq DNS & DHCP → Hosts) produced a persistent, reproducible `NXDOMAIN` on every query, despite every layer of the stack checking out correct in isolation. Full elimination chain, each step ruling out one explanation before moving to the next:

- **Not a connectivity/firewall issue:** an initially-proposed `ping` test to the zone's own `.1` address was flawed, not the finding itself — the standing "Block OPNsense Control Plane" rule intentionally drops all non-DNS traffic including ICMP to the interface's own IP, so that ping failure was expected behavior, consistent with every other zone. All diagnostic queries were run as `drill @127.0.0.1 -p 53053 ...` directly on the firewall's own loopback, which never crosses an interface boundary — firewall/zone rules are structurally incapable of affecting this traffic, confirmed explicitly when the operator asked.
- **Not a stale dnsmasq process:** a process found via `sockstat` was confirmed to predate several restart attempts (`ps -o lstart` showed it running since before a `configctl dnsmasq restart`). A genuine kill-and-respawn was confirmed (new PID, verified via `pgrep`) — the failure persisted identically against a provably fresh process.
- **Not the hosts file content:** `/var/etc/dnsmasq-hosts` was checked and re-checked, including immediately after a fresh restart, and consistently contained the correct, correctly-formatted entries.
- **Not file permissions:** `/var/etc/dnsmasq-hosts` is world-readable (`rw-r--r--`); dnsmasq runs as `nobody` and can read it.
- **Not `home.arpa` RFC 8375 special-case handling:** a plausible theory (dnsmasq/Unbound both have built-in handling for this reserved special-use domain) was tested and disproven directly — a brand-new throwaway Host Override under a completely unrelated domain (`test01.test.lab`) failed identically.
- **Not a stuck process/kernel/cache state:** a full cyber-fw01 reboot (operator's instinct from the start, deliberately held off on until config-level causes were exhausted, per the project's root-cause-before-reboot discipline) changed nothing — `test01.test.lab` and the hybrid entries failed identically before and after.
- **Not file-loading specifically:** the inline `host-record=` directive (parsed directly from the main config, no external file involved) was tested as a throwaway addition and failed identically to the file-based `addn-hosts` entries.

**Root cause: Dnsmasq's entire static-host-record resolution engine does not function on this OPNsense 26.7.5 build** — neither `addn-hosts` nor `host-record=`, for any domain, under any tested condition. Checking Unbound's own Host Overrides page (empty — "No results found") and the Dnsmasq Hosts tab (only this session's own test entries) further showed that **no static host record of any kind predates tonight anywhere in this project** — meaning this was very likely the first real end-to-end test of hostname-based forward DNS resolution via the Dnsmasq backend in the project's history, not a regression. Earlier zones' apparent "working" DNS most likely reflected DHCP lease *assignment* succeeding (which is unaffected) rather than anyone having actually resolved a hostname by FQDN against this code path before.

**Fix applied:** moved static DNS off Dnsmasq entirely. Added one Unbound Host Override (Services → Unbound DNS → Overrides): Host `cyber-dmz01`, Domain `hybrid.cyberrange.home.arpa`, Type A, IP `10.10.70.10`, PTR enabled, with `www`, `blog`, and `portal-dev` (same domain) added via that same entry's built-in **Aliases** field rather than as separate records. Verified via `drill @127.0.0.1 ...` (port 53, Unbound) on cyber-fw01 and independently via `dig +short` from VM370 itself querying its DHCP-assigned DNS server — all four names resolve to `10.10.70.10`. The dead `cyber-dmz01`/`test01` Dnsmasq Hosts entries and the throwaway `host-record=probe01.test.lab` config line were removed/flushed afterward. **This is now the standing DNS convention for the project going forward** — see §5.3 item 7.

**Two further, unrelated defects surfaced and documented but not yet fixed (low priority, no longer blocking since static records now bypass Dnsmasq entirely):**
- The generated `dnsmasq.conf` is missing a `domain=apps.cyberrange.home.arpa,...` directive for VLAN 40/CYBER_APPS — present for every other zone, absent for this one. Root cause not pursued (Dnsmasq's static-record path is being retired for DNS purposes regardless).
- Two typos in Unbound's custom forwarding list: `apps.cyberrange.hoome.arpa` (extra "o") and `quarantine.cyyberrange.home.arpa` (double "y"). Harmless now that forwarding to Dnsmasq's broken static-record engine is no longer the resolution path for any zone's hostnames, but worth correcting the next time that page is touched.

**VM370 OS build, hardening, and DNS infrastructure CLOSED 4 Oct 2026.** Next: Docker + nginx reverse-proxy web layer (main site, decoy WordPress, SQLi app), then VM371.

**11.3.1g VM370 (cyber-dmz01) web layer build, a second platform defect found and fixed, and first full CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID validation (5 Oct 2026)**

With OS/hardening/DNS closed (§11.3.1f), built the Docker/nginx web layer and ran the zone's first real end-to-end attack-path validation.

**Docker and web stack build:**

1. **Docker installed** (Step 5b). Operator explicitly chose to add `cyberadmin` to the `docker` group for convenience, judged acceptable given the host's disposable nature and existing SSH scoping to CYBER_ATTACK only.
2. **Three design decisions confirmed before building** (Step 5c): the SQLi app would be PHP + MySQL (not a canned vulnerable-app image); the seeded credential would be decided now rather than left as a placeholder; this pass would be HTTP-only, no TLS yet.
3. **Seeded credential computed as a real NTLM hash**, not a placeholder: `svc_legacysync` / `Changeme123` → `8091fee1f3890584904bd7d5cea1240e`, intended to become a real AD account on VM371 later so the hash cracked here will actually work there. Computed with `pycryptodome`'s `Crypto.Hash.MD4` (Python's built-in `hashlib.new('md4', ...)` fails on this environment's OpenSSL build with `UnsupportedDigestmodError`) and verified against a known NTLM test vector before trusting it.
4. **Decoy credentials added per explicit operator request** — "so it's not just one straight here's-your-login to windows." Final `sync_accounts` table seeded with 8 rows: the one real/live credential (`svc_legacysync`, hash above) plus 7 decoys of varying plausibility — several flagged `STALE`/disabled with distinct, differently-aged NTLM hashes (`svc_backup`, `tnguyen`, `rpatel`, `kadams`), and three sharing the empty-password NTLM hash (`31d6cfe0d16ae931b73c59d7e0c089c0`) under `OK` status (`jdoe`, `asmith`, `mwilson`) as a second-layer trap for anyone who cracks-then-trusts the first hash that comes back clean.
5. **Docker Compose stack built**: nginx reverse proxy doing name-based virtual hosting, plus one container per site — static main site (`main-site`, nginx:alpine), WordPress + MariaDB (`wordpress`/`wordpress-db`), and the custom PHP 8.2 + MySQL 8.0 vulnerable app (`portal-dev`/`portal-dev-db`). Only nginx's port 80 published to the host; all inter-container traffic stays on the default Compose bridge network.
6. **Three vhosts resolved via the Unbound Host Override's Aliases field** (§11.3.1f): `www`/`cyber-dmz01` → main site "Meridian Capital Partners," `blog` → decoy WordPress "Meridian Insights," `portal-dev` → the vulnerable "Legacy AD Sync Status Checker" app. The app's `search.php` endpoint is intentionally vulnerable: raw string concatenation of `$_GET['user']` into the SQL query, with the raw DB error echoed back to the client on failure — a standard error-based/UNION-based SQLi target.

**Temporary kali01 → VM370 SSH deviation, opened and fully reverted:**

Operator requested a temporary SSH path from kali01 (10.10.1.85, native LAN, has RustDesk) into VM370 (10.10.70.10) purely for copy/paste convenience while building the compose files by hand, specifically to avoid adding RustDesk to VM371 later. Correctly scoped once the direction and interface were worked out (not CYBER_ATTACK — kali01 sits on the native LAN interface, a different host and zone from kali-attack/VM310): a LAN-interface pass rule on cyber-fw01 (source `KALI01` alias, destination `10.10.70.10`, TCP/22, positioned above the RFC1918 block) plus a matching VM370 UFW rule scoped to `10.10.1.85`. Used to build the compose files, then **both sides fully reverted and confirmed removed** — the OPNsense LAN rule deleted (confirmed: no other LAN-side rule besides the standard set remains) and the UFW rule deleted (confirmed via `ufw status numbered` showing only the original 5 rules).

**Platform defect #1 — MySQL 8.0 crash-looping on CPU microarchitecture:**

`portal-dev-db` crash-looped with `Fatal glibc error: CPU does not support x86-64-v2`. Root cause: VM370 was created without an explicit `--cpu` flag, defaulting Proxmox to a conservative baseline (kvm64/qemu64) lacking x86-64-v2 instructions (SSE4.2 etc.) that MySQL 8.0's official image requires at the glibc level, even though the physical host CPU supports it. **Fixed:** `qm shutdown 370` → `qm set 370 --cpu host` → `qm start 370` (a full power cycle is required; a guest-level reboot does not pick up the change). Accepted as fine for this disposable lab host since migration portability to a different physical CPU isn't a concern here. Confirmed via `docker compose ps` showing all 6 containers `Running`/`Up` with no further restarts.

**End-to-end validation from kali-attack, and platform defect #2 — missing static IP:**

Per operator's explicit confirmation, **all scenario validation has been and will continue to be run from kali-attack (kali02/VM310)**, the correct CYBER_ATTACK-zone host — kali01 was used only for the file-copy convenience above and played no further role once reverted.

The first validation pass from kali-attack surfaced a real, unrelated defect: `dig` resolved all three vhosts correctly, but `ping`, `curl`, and `nc -zv` to `10.10.70.10` all failed (100% ICMP loss, TCP connection timed out) — despite OPNsense's live firewall log showing the §7.4.4 attack-path rule correctly passing the traffic. Diagnosed layer by layer:

- **Firewall ruled out first:** Firewall → Diagnostics → States, captured live during a test, showed a genuine bidirectional state for the flow, correctly matched to the attack-path-exception rule both directions, source address still the real `10.10.10.177` (unchanged — confirmed no NAT rewrite by also checking Firewall → NAT → Source NAT, which showed Automatic mode generating mappings for the `wan` interface only, not inter-VLAN traffic). The firewall was doing exactly what it should.
- **Root cause found via `ip a` on VM370**: the VLAN70-facing NIC (`ens18`) was holding a **DHCP-leased** address, `10.10.70.107/24` — not the intended static `10.10.70.10`. `cat /etc/netplan/*.yaml` confirmed why: the `ens18` stanza was `dhcp4: true` with **no static address block at all** — unlike `ens19` (VLAN 71), which did have one. The static `10.10.70.10` had simply never been written into the config; this wasn't a regression or drift, it was never there, masked until this was the first real cross-VLAN reachability test of this host.
- **Fixed:** rewrote the `ens18` netplan stanza — `dhcp4: false`, static `10.10.70.10/24`, explicit default route via `10.10.70.1`, and an explicit nameserver (`10.10.70.1`) to replace DNS info the host had previously been getting incidentally from the now-removed DHCP lease. Applied via `netplan apply`, confirmed via `ip a show ens18` (static, no `dynamic` flag).

**Full validation, clean, from kali-attack:**
- `ping -c 3 10.10.70.10` — 3/3 received, 0% loss
- `curl` to all three vhosts — main site, decoy WordPress, and the vulnerable app (live DB query for `jdoe` returning correct seeded data) all rendered correctly
- `gobuster dir` against both the main site and `portal-dev` — both completed cleanly at 100% progress with no hangs or timeouts, confirming sustained throughput over the attack path, not just single-request reachability (`portal-dev` also showed Apache's default `.htaccess`/`.htpasswd`/`server-status` protections correctly returning 403)

This is the first real live-host validation of the §7.4.4 CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID attack-path exception (previously validated by rule-order inspection only) — now fully confirmed positive.

**VM370 web layer build, both platform defects, and full attack-path validation CLOSED 5 Oct 2026.** Next: build VM371 (`cyber-corpsrv01`).

**11.3.1h Gate 8G3 item 3 (hybrid zone) — VM371 (cyber-corpsrv01) build, scenario-account wiring, and pivot validation — CLOSED 7 Oct 2026**

**Operator-confirmed closure. No console evidence captured** (range security restriction prevents uploading screenshots/console output off the range) — logged from the agreed build plan plus operator confirmation of success, a deliberate and noted exception to the project's standard screenshot-backed evidence discipline. With VM371 built and the full relayed privilege check passing, the Windows/Linux combination zone (Gate 8G3 roadmap item 3, §11.3.1b) is now **fully closed** — both VM370 (§11.3.1f/§11.3.1g) and VM371 complete, capstone path end-to-end live.

**Base build (single-homed, by design):**
- Created mirroring DC01's spec — 1 socket / 4 cores / 8192 MiB / 80 GiB, UEFI (OVMF) + q35 + vTPM 2.0, VirtIO SCSI, QEMU agent, ISOs from `NAS-ISO-Repo-CyberRange`. **Single NIC only**, `virtio,bridge=vmbr1,tag=71,firewall=1` — one leg on VLAN 71 (CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL) and nowhere else, unlike dual-homed VM370. NIC-on-tag-71-only confirmed via `qm config 371 | grep net` before install.
- **Windows Server 2022 Standard (Desktop Experience)**, VirtIO storage driver loaded from `virtio-win.iso` at disk-selection (same as DC01/VM321/VM322). Hostname `cyber-corpsrv01` — a member server, not a workstation.
- **Static networking, deliberately not pointed at the gateway for DNS:** IP `10.10.71.11/24` (`.10` on VLAN 71 is VM370's second NIC), gateway `10.10.71.1`, **DNS `10.10.20.10` (DC01, not OPNsense)** — the per-host-DNS pattern from v1.35 (VM321/VM322) that avoids the DNS/registry-change approval trigger. Resolves because the §11.3.1e `CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL → CYBER_WINDOWS` AD-ports pass already carries TCP/UDP 53.
- **Domain-joined** to `corp.cyberrange.local` (same process as VM321/VM322), rebooted, `whoami /fqdn` confirmed per operator.
- **`hardened-baseline` snapshot** before any scenario/hardening config — same clean → harden → snapshot discipline as every other host.

**Scenario-content wiring (sequenced after the base build, per the prior-session plan):**
- `svc_legacysync` created as a real AD account on DC01, matching the credential seeded into VM370's SQLi app in §11.3.1g — `svc_legacysync` / `Changeme123`, NTLM `8091fee1f3890584904bd7d5cea1240e`. The hash cracked out of the web layer now authenticates for real against this forest, closing the loop the §11.3.1g seed opened.
- WinRM access granted to `svc_legacysync` on VM371.
- **`SeImpersonatePrivilege` granted directly** to the account (operator's chosen mechanism, not via a real service), enabling the Potato-family escalation stage once the account is reached via evil-winrm.

**Pivot validation — full relayed path, not local-only:**
- VM370's temporary chisel-test UFW rule replaced with the permanent, zone-scoped rule: `ufw allow from 10.10.10.0/24 to any port 8080 proto tcp` (comment `legacy monitoring agent`) — open only to sources in CYBER_ATTACK, filtered/closed to every other zone under UFW default-deny. A **source-scoped** asymmetry, not an inbound/outbound one; no OPNsense change, one-way isolation posture intact.
- VM370 ran `chisel server --port 8080` (the compromised-foothold host is the server — "attacker drops a tool and pivots from the box they already own").
- kali-attack ran `chisel client 10.10.70.10:8080 5985:10.10.71.11:5985`, then `evil-winrm -i 127.0.0.1 -u svc_legacysync -p Changeme123`.
- Inside the relayed session, `whoami /priv` showed **`SeImpersonatePrivilege` Enabled** over the full path (kali-attack → chisel tunnel → VM370 → VM371 WinRM), confirming tunnel and privilege grant end-to-end.

**Design decision folded in (deferred, not built here):** a dedicated C2/beaconing host for frameworks such as Sliver is confirmed as its own future box in its own zone (TBD), not bolted onto this web-app-compromise scenario. VM370 remaining a chisel *server* (not an outbound-dialing client) kept the one-way CYBER_ATTACK → CYBER_HYBRID design intact; a true reverse/beaconing topology would need a deliberate new `CYBER_HYBRID → CYBER_ATTACK` rule and is out of scope until the Sliver box is designed.

**Scope of this closure:** covers the *build* plus *reachability + privilege* validation. The remaining capstone stages — Potato-family local escalation to SYSTEM, BloodHound path discovery, and the walk to Domain Admin on the existing forest — are now **available for the operator to run as the capstone red-team exercise**; they are practice activity, not build-validation steps, and are not claimed as completed here.

**11.3.1i Gate 8G3 item 4 — Analysis zone content: VM360 rebuilt as a GUI analyst workstation + MITRE ATT&CK Navigator — CLOSED 7 Oct 2026**

With Navigator deployed and rendering, Gate 8G3 roadmap items 1–4 (§11.3.1b) are all closed; next is item 5, the SIEM zone (VM350/351).

**Decision — rebuild rather than retrofit, and view locally rather than expose.** VM360 had only ever been a headless connectivity-validation probe carrying no tooling. Two access models were weighed for reaching Navigator's web UI: (a) SSH local-forward from kali01, or (b) rebuild VM360 with a desktop and view the UI on-box.
