  - OPNsense, CYBER_ANALYSIS interface, above the PROTECTED_NETWORKS block: pass TCP from `10.10.60.141` (VM360) to `10.10.50.149` (VM350) ports 443 (Wazuh), 5000 (Monkey), 8888 (Caldera).
  - VM350 UFW: `allow from 10.10.60.141 to any port 443,5000,8888 proto tcp` (comment `VM360 dashboard access`).
  - **Validation (all pass):** positive — all three UIs load and log in from VM360 (Wazuh Overview "no agents yet", Monkey Getting Started, Caldera 5.3.0 dashboard). Negative — from kali-attack (VLAN 10) all three return `000`/timeout (attack zone cannot see the SIEM). Port-scope — from VM360, `nc` to VM350:22 times out (only the three ports pass). UFW scope confirmed single-host + three-ports via `ufw status verbose`.
  - Default Caldera `red`/`blue` creds changed off `admin` before this pass went live.
- **8H.2 — Wazuh agent telemetry (monitored zones → VM350:1514/1515) — IN PROGRESS; Linux zone proven end-to-end 8 Oct 2026.** Monitored set confirmed: Windows VLAN 20, Linux VLAN 30, Apps VLAN 40, Hybrid VLAN 70/71 (attack zone excluded — attack boxes aren't monitored; SIEM self-monitors locally; analysis zone optional). Ports TCP 1514 (events) + 1515 (enrollment), subnet-scoped. Reusable aliases created: `SIEM_MANAGER` = `10.10.50.149`, `WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS` = `1514,1515`.
  - **Linux zone (VLAN 30) — CLOSED 8 Oct 2026 (first-zone proof):** OPNsense pass on CYBER_LINUX (`10.10.30.0/24` → `SIEM_MANAGER` ports `WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS`, above PROTECTED_NETWORKS) + VM350 UFW `allow from 10.10.30.0/24 to any port 1514,1515 proto tcp`. Enrolled `wazuh-agent` 4.14.8 on VM332 `cyber-ubuntu01` (`10.10.30.105`) → shows **Active** in the dashboard. **Detection validated end-to-end:** vuln-detection inventory populated from the agent; an SSH brute-force from kali-attack (VLAN 10) against VM332 surfaced in Threat Hunting as `sshd: authentication failed` (rule 5760, lvl 5) plus the correlated **rule 2502, lvl 10** ("User missed the password more than one time"). Negative test: kali-attack `nc` to `10.10.50.149:1514` times out (telemetry pass scoped to the Linux zone, not the attack zone). *(Note: Threat Hunting is the renamed "Security events" module; FIM only logs monitored file changes — set the dashboard time range to Last 24h.)*
  - **Windows zone (VLAN 20) — CLOSED 8 Oct 2026.** OPNsense pass on CYBER_WINDOWS (`10.10.20.0/24` → `SIEM_MANAGER`:`WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS`) + VM350 UFW allow. Enrolled Windows agents via the MSI + wizard PowerShell one-liner (`WAZUH_MANAGER=10.10.50.149`, per-host `WAZUH_AGENT_NAME`): `cyber-dc01` (`10.10.20.10`, Server 2025), `cyber-ws01` (`10.10.20.113`, Win10), `cyber-ws02` (`10.10.20.131`, Win11) — all **Active** with alerts flowing. *(Note: ws01/ws02 names were entered swapped at enrollment — cosmetic; agent names bind at enrollment and are changed by remove-and-re-enroll, e.g. `agent-auth.exe -A <name>`, not in place. Sysmon for richer ATT&CK telemetry is a deferred closeout item, §10.)*
  - **Apps zone (VLAN 40) — CLOSED 8 Oct 2026.** OPNsense pass on CYBER_APPS (`10.10.40.0/24` → `SIEM_MANAGER`:`WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS`) + VM350 UFW allow. Linux agent on VM340 `cyber-apps01` (`10.10.40.121`) → Active with host events showing; negative test verified. *(DVWA/Juice Shop run in Docker, so the host agent sees host events but not the container web-app logs — detecting web attacks against the apps needs the Wazuh Docker-listener integration on this host, tracked as a §10 deferred item.)*
  - **Hybrid zone (VLAN 70/71) — CLOSED 8 Oct 2026 → 8H.2 COMPLETE.** Passes on both CYBER_HYBRID (`10.10.70.0/24`) and CYBER_HYBRID_INTERNAL (`10.10.71.0/24`) → `SIEM_MANAGER`:`WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS`, + VM350 UFW allows. Agents: `cyber-dmz01` (VM370, `10.10.70.10`, Linux, via its VLAN 70 default route) and `cyber-corpsrv01` (VM371, `10.10.71.11`, Windows) — both **Active**. The VLAN 71 pass is the **first outbound exception from the internal-only zone** — scoped to the SIEM manager on agent ports only, does not open VLAN 71 to inbound (consistent with §11.3.1e). Two real gotchas this zone surfaced: (1) VM371 is internet-less, so its agent MSI (version-matched **4.14.8**, not the wizard's newer 4.18.8) was staged from VM370 over VLAN 71 via a temporary `python3 -m http.server` (which also needed a temporary VM370 UFW allow for port 8000, since VM370's own default-deny-inbound blocked it — removed after); (2) the CYBER_HYBRID pass initially put `WAZUH_AGENT_PORTS` in the **source** port field instead of **destination**, so it matched nothing (agent traffic originates from a random high port) and the default-deny caught it — `nc` to `:1514/:1515` timed out until the port alias was moved to the destination field. **All four monitored zones (Linux, Windows, Apps, Hybrid) now report; 8H.2 is complete.**
- **8H.3 — BAS lateral-movement — IN PROGRESS (design agreed 9 Oct 2026).** The highest-blast-radius direction (SIEM ↔ target zones). **Decision: no standing BAS cross-zone passes.** Instead, each scenario is a **standing operating procedure** that opens a tightly-scoped path, runs the exercise, closes the path, and resets the target to a clean snapshot — captured in the project doc **`claude/SOP-BAS-Scenarios.md`**. Tool directions differ: Caldera = targets→VM350:8888 (sandcat beacon-back, inbound to SIEM, lowest risk); Infection Monkey = agents (which run **on** targets)→Island:5000 — **the Island does NOT attack targets, so there is no VM350→targets Monkey rule**; intra-zone propagation is L2 (no rule), cross-zone propagation is an optional/advanced per-scenario add; ART = VM350→targets (WinRM/SSH remoting), built later. **Cleanup reality:** both tools have best-effort self-cleanup (Caldera per-ability cleanup on operation termination; Monkey agents self-delete) but neither is pristine-reliable — so **snapshot revert is the guaranteed reset** and is the final phase of every SOP (leaning on the range's disposable-by-design snapshots). **Model refined 9 Oct 2026 (operator):** BAS firewall rules are **pre-built and left disabled** (enable/disable per scenario rather than create/delete), the matching VM350 UFW allows are **standing** (OPNsense is the sole L3 hop so a standing UFW allow is inert while the OPNsense rule is disabled — UFW has no per-rule toggle), and each monitored box carries a **`monitored-baseline`** snapshot (Wazuh agent + auditd/Sysmon) that is the revert target. **Foundations-first sequencing agreed:** (1) monitoring + `monitored-baseline` per box, (2) pre-built disabled OPNsense BAS rule set + standing UFW, then (3) the SOPs are short enable/run/disable/revert procedures. The Caldera rule catalog (5 zone rules) and the per-box monitoring-baseline table live in `claude/SOP-BAS-Scenarios.md` §0. **First SOP written and run: SOP-1 Caldera → Linux zone** (VM332 monitored-baseline done) (open CYBER_LINUX→VM350:8888 + UFW, deploy sandcat on VM332, run operation, observe in Wazuh, cleanup, close path, `qm rollback`). Remaining SOPs: Caldera → Windows/Apps/Hybrid, Monkey → each zone, ART → each zone. **8H.3 FOUNDATIONS COMPLETE 10 Oct 2026:** all 10 monitored boxes carry `monitored-baseline` snapshots (agent + auditd/Sysmon); the 10 BAS OPNsense rules (5 Caldera + 5 Monkey Tier-1) are pre-built and disabled, with the 10 standing VM350 UFW allows in place (`claude/SOP-BAS-Scenarios.md` §0). SOP execution is now enable/run/disable/revert; the §10 Sysmon/auditd/re-baseline items are resolved.

**Operational note:** VM350 (`10.10.50.149`) and VM360 (`10.10.60.141`) are on dynamic DHCP leases that the 8H rules and all future Wazuh agent configs pin to. **Action: add DHCP static reservations** for both (VM350 MAC `bc:24:11:a1:0a:17`) in OPNsense so the addresses can't drift under the rules (operator agreed 8 Oct 2026).

### 11.6 Gate 9 — fail-closed exercise and closeout

Schedule a separate maintenance gate with Proxmox console access and both management paths available before interrupting OPNsense.
Prove selected cyber guests lose public and protected reachability when OPNsense is unavailable and do not discover another gateway.
Restore OPNsense; confirm DHCP, DNS, NAT, firewall logs, and management access; then create fresh VM and configuration backups.
Publish the final as-built architecture, register, roadmap, backlog, firewall matrix, and recovery catalog.

#### 11.6.1 Gate 9 execution record — **CLOSED 14 Oct 2026**

The fail-closed exercise was run as a bounded maintenance gate and **passed**, demonstrating the §1.1 containment invariant under real interruption.

- **Pre-flight (Phase 0):** Proxmox host console confirmed reachable independently of OPNsense (lifeline); noVNC console login verified on each test guest **before** the outage; baseline captured; firewall safety snapshot available (`pre-gate9`-class) alongside the already-hashed XML export.
- **Test scope:** three guests across three zones — **VM310** (Kali02, VLAN 10 attack), **VM332** (cyber-ubuntu01, VLAN 30 Linux), **VM340** (cyber-apps01, VLAN 40 Apps). All three verified normal connectivity (public HTTPS up) **before** cyber-fw01 (VM300) shutdown.
- **Phase 1 → 2 (OPNsense DOWN):** with cyber-fw01 (VM300) shut down, all three guests **lost reachability to google**, **failed ping to 1.1.1.1**, **obtained no new IP routes** (no alternate gateway discovered, no second NIC/vmbr0), and **kept Proxmox / CGNAT / link-local unreachable** exactly as expected. Internet went down and stayed down with no bypass path — fail-closed confirmed.
- **Phase 3 (restore):** cyber-fw01 restarted; DHCP, DNS, NAT, firewall logs, and management access all confirmed restored; guests returned to normal connectivity (public HTTPS back up).
- **Phase 4 (closeout):** fresh post-exercise OPNsense configuration **XML exported**; Gate 9 recorded here. Final as-built records (architecture, register, firewall matrix, recovery catalog) are current in this document.

**Result: Gate 9 PASS — the cyberrange fails closed when cyber-fw01 is unavailable, with no guest bypass of OPNsense. The core build arc (Gates 1–9) is complete.** Next: the operator's planned additional scenario.

## 12 Standard validation and acceptance matrix

### 12.1 Linux active-purpose validation commands

Run this common block exactly as written. It contains no placeholders. Public HTTPS is the authoritative Internet test. The OPNsense control-plane test is separate because its .1 address is zone-specific.

```bash
hostnamectl --static
ip -4 -brief address
ip route
getent ahostsv4 example.com

if command -v curl >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  curl -4 -sS --connect-timeout 10 -o /dev/null \
    -w 'HTTPS_STATUS=%{http_code}\n' https://www.google.com/
else
  echo 'INFO: curl is not installed'
fi

if timeout 5 bash -c '</dev/tcp/192.168.10.2/8006' 2>/dev/null; then
  echo 'FAIL: Proxmox reachable'
else
  echo 'PASS: Proxmox unreachable'
fi

if timeout 5 bash -c '</dev/tcp/100.100.100.100/443' 2>/dev/null; then
  echo 'FAIL: CGNAT/Tailscale destination reachable'
else
  echo 'PASS: CGNAT/Tailscale unreachable'
fi

if timeout 5 bash -c '</dev/tcp/169.254.169.254/80' 2>/dev/null; then
  echo 'FAIL: link-local destination reachable'
else
  echo 'PASS: link-local unreachable'
fi

if ip -6 -o addr show scope global | grep -q .; then
  echo 'FAIL: global IPv6 address present'
else
  echo 'PASS: no global IPv6 address'
fi
```

For the OPNsense control-plane test, use the exact .1 address for the guest's zone and TCP 443. VM310 uses 10.10.10.1; VM360 uses 10.10.60.1. Future zone runbooks must provide the exact command rather than a placeholder. **Now that PROTECTED_NETWORKS is corrected (22 Sep 2026), a CGNAT/Tailscale test against 100.100.100.100 still only confirms a single address within the range. A more thorough validation should also probe an address outside the old, incorrect 100.64.0.0/16 but inside the correct 100.64.0.0/10, e.g. 100.100.0.1, to positively confirm the wider range is now genuinely blocked — this has not yet been run and is a good first check under Gate 8G1 step 3.**

### 12.2 Firewall log correlation

| Destination | Expected result | Expected label / policy path |
|---|---|---|
| Zone gateway .1 TCP 443 | Blocked | Block OPNsense Control Plane |
| 192.168.10.2 TCP 8006 | Blocked | Block Private and Inter-Zone Networks using PROTECTED_NETWORKS |
| 100.100.100.100 TCP 443 | Blocked | PROTECTED_NETWORKS block |
| 169.254.169.254 TCP 80 | Blocked | PROTECTED_NETWORKS block |
| Public HTTPS | Allowed | Bottom active-purpose Internet pass, then automatic WAN Source NAT |
| Any IPv6 upstream | Unavailable / blocked | No approved IPv6 configuration or pass |

### 12.3 Acceptance decision

Pass only when DNS and public HTTPS succeed, every protected destination fails, IPv6 is absent, and OPNsense logs match the intended rules.
A failed raw TCP attempt to 1.1.1.1:443 does not override a successful curl -4 HTTPS transaction to a normal public site.
A printed PASS or EXPECTED BLOCK is provisional until the corresponding firewall log is reviewed for source, destination, action, and label.
If a prohibited destination succeeds, stop testing, preserve evidence, and power off the guest if continued connection creates containment risk. Do not weaken policy while it remains connected.

---

The second GPU is a GTX 970 4 GB (not the originally-referenced 8 GB card); the host console runs on the RX 7700 XT (slot 1; the board has no primary-GPU selector), which frees the GTX 970 for non-AI GPU workloads (GPU-accelerated password cracking, e.g. hashcat) via exclusive vfio passthrough — a tracked open build item (§10), not yet set up.

> **AI SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE (finalized 22 Sep 2026)**
> - The cyberrange AI system (attacking and defending model support) is hosted entirely on the cyberrange Proxmox node and never crosses to the separate homelab AI node, to prevent leakage of purposefully malicious/vulnerable cyberrange content into homelab-adjacent infrastructure.
> - attack-ai (VM311) lives in VLAN 10 with VM310; defense-ai (VM351) lives in VLAN 50 with the future SIEM host. Neither AI guest has a cross-zone path to the other; any future interaction would be a narrow, logged Gate 8H scenario rule, not standing infrastructure.
> - Both are LXC containers rather than full VMs specifically so they can share the 12 GB GPU concurrently via cgroup device passthrough. This trades a hardware-virtualization boundary for a shared-kernel boundary. That trade was evaluated and accepted: a container escape and a hypervisor escape both terminate at the same place (the cyberrange Proxmox host), so this does not introduce a new blast-radius class — only a higher-probability path to an already-accepted worst case.
> - The actually load-bearing control for leakage-to-homelab risk is NOT the VLAN/OPNsense segmentation (which only covers vmbr1 guest traffic) but the cyberrange Proxmox host's own management-plane exposure on 192.168.10.119/vmbr0, which sits on the trusted subnet. This is being formally audited and hardened under Gate 8G1.

Models from small/unofficial publishers are treated as unaudited weights, same trust posture as any other untrusted binary, and are scoped to the disposable/rebuildable VLAN 10 or VLAN 50 guests accordingly.
>
> **DNS, NAT, AND FIREWALL**
> - Dnsmasq serves LAN and all seven cyber interfaces on port 53053 and provides the approved DHCP pools.
> - Unbound listens on port 53 and forwards the seven cyber zones and seven /24 reverse zones to 127.0.0.1:53053. Preserve the existing polaris.home.arpa forward. Do not create ad.polaris or a parent cyberrange.home.arpa forward.

Treat this as resolved, not open.**
> - The legacy RFC1918_NETWORKS alias lacks CGNAT/Tailscale and link-local coverage and is insufficient for the active-purpose protected-network block.
> - On CYBER_ATTACK, CYBER_WINDOWS, CYBER_LINUX, CYBER_APPS, CYBER_SIEM, and CYBER_ANALYSIS, the manual IPv4 order beneath automatic rules is: 1) Pass TCP/UDP from interface network to This Firewall port 53 for DNS. 2) Block and log IPv4 any from interface network to This Firewall. 3) Block and log IPv4 any from interface network to PROTECTED_NETWORKS. 4) Pass IPv4 any from interface network to any for public Internet. The Internet pass must remain below both blocks. Confirmed via GUI on all six zones, 22 Sep 2026.
> - CYBER_QUARANTINE remains closed: no manual DNS pass, no Internet pass, explicit blocks plus implicit default deny. Second rule destination confirmed via GUI as RFC1918_NETWORKS, 22 Sep 2026.
> - Cyber guests should have Internet through OPNsense during normal operation and fail closed when OPNsense is unavailable. Inability to ping the .1 gateway is expected because the OPNsense control plane is blocked; the .1 address is still the correct gateway.

The operator applied the alias, verified all five runtime entries, and repeated the test. Proxmox then blocked while Internet HTTPS remained allowed.

For boundary aliases use: Save → Apply on Aliases → verify runtime table → edit or activate dependent rules → Apply firewall rules → clear only relevant states if required → validate positive and negative outcomes. This same procedure was followed for the 22 Sep 2026 PROTECTED_NETWORKS content correction (127.16.0.0/12 → 172.16.0.0/12; 100.64.0.0/16 → 100.64.0.0/10), now closed and re-verified.

A full elimination chain (not a firewall/loopback issue; not a stale process — confirmed via fresh PID; not file content or permissions; not `home.arpa` special-casing — disproven with an unrelated throwaway test domain; not stuck kernel/cache state — a full reboot changed nothing; not file-loading specifically — the inline `host-record=` directive failed identically) isolated the root cause: **Dnsmasq's entire static-host-record engine does not function on this OPNsense 26.7.5 build**, for any domain, under any tested condition. Checking Unbound's own Host Overrides (empty) and Dnsmasq's Hosts tab (only this session's test entries) revealed no static host record predates this build anywhere in the project — likely the first real end-to-end test of this code path, not a regression. **Fix: static DNS records now live in Unbound Host Overrides instead of Dnsmasq**, a standing convention change for the project going forward (new §5.3 item 7); Dnsmasq remains in use for DHCP only. Verified working both from cyber-fw01 and from VM370 itself. Also surfaced two unrelated, lower-priority defects not yet fixed: a missing `domain=apps.cyberrange.home.arpa` directive, and two typos in Unbound's custom forwarding list (`hoome`, `cyyberrange`). Reassessed that `tailscale ping` tests Tailscale's own control-plane NAT-traversal handshake, not real IP-packet delivery to the tun device, so its earlier success does not conflict with this result. With OS routing, OS firewall, and the app all now confirmed clean, leading hypothesis shifted to `tailscaled`'s own internal netstack/packet filter blocking delivery before packets reach `tailscale0`.
